How answers are graded:
How answers are graded

- Each attempt is compared with your answer and marked right or wrong. The grade is on the whole attempt; the final answer shown is picked out automatically as a guide.
- Numbers match when they are the same value written differently: 0.675, 67.5% and 67.5 percentage points all match, and so do different scales (thousand, million, billion), thousands separators, currency symbols and numbers written as words.
- If the question asks for a particular format (a percentage, a number of decimal places, a unit, a rounding or an order), the attempt must meet it. If it asks for none, any equivalent form is accepted.
- For an ordered list, a title, honorific or article added to an entry is accepted if the order is right either with it or without it.
- Only the value the attempt finally commits to counts. Values reached while working, or considered and set aside, do not. An attempt that offers several values without choosing one is wrong, even if one of them is right.

Full answer
-----------

The submission deadline was 12 pm Monday 13 July 2026, so treat that as the “final decision” date.

Proposed caps (Document 1, Table X1):

- Lowest: 0.50 % (domestic in‑person)
- Highest: 1.50 % (foreign‑issued online)

Constraints:

- Mastercard (Document 10): 9 months for domestic changes; 12 months for cross‑border (foreign‑issued) changes, measured from the date of final announcement.
- Westpac (Document 14): minimum 6‑month implementation; effective date should align to the 1st of a month and avoid the Dec–Feb period; Westpac cites 1 March 2027 at the earliest.

Applying both:

Lowest cap (0.50 %, domestic)

- Mastercard: 13 Jul 2026 + 9 months = 13 Apr 2027
- Westpac: round up to the next 1st of the month after that (and outside Dec–Feb) = 1 May 2027
- Days after 13 Jul 2026: 292 days

Highest cap (1.50 %, foreign‑issued / cross‑border)

- Mastercard: 13 Jul 2026 + 12 months = 13 Jul 2027
- Westpac: round up to the next 1st of the month = 1 Aug 2027
- Days after 13 Jul 2026: 384 days

So, in order (lowest, then highest):

- 0.50 % cap: could first start 292 days later (1 May 2027)
- 1.50 % cap: could first start 384 days later (1 Aug 2027)

Full answer
-----------

The submission deadline (and hypothetical final-decision date) is 12:00 pm Monday 13 July 2026 (Document 1, §1.21).

Proposed caps

- Lowest: 0.50% (domestic in-person) → domestic card change
- Highest: 1.50% (foreign-issued online) → cross-border change

Constraints to satisfy

- Mastercard (Document 10):
- Domestic: minimum 9 months from announcement
- Cross-border: minimum 12 months from announcement
- Westpac (Document 14, §3.21):
- Minimum 6 months (so no earlier than 13 Jan 2027)
- Effective date no earlier than 1 March 2027
- Must align to a first-of-month effective date

Earliest permissible start dates

- Domestic (0.50%): Must be on/after 13 Apr 2027 and on/after 1 Mar 2027, first of month → 1 May 2027
- 13 Jul 2026 → 1 May 2027 = 292 days

Full answer
-----------

The submission deadline was 12 pm Monday 13 July 2026, so the “final decision” date is 13 July 2026.

Proposed caps (Document 1):

- Lowest: 0.50 % (domestic, in‑person)
- Highest: 1.50 % (foreign‑issued, online)

Requirements:

- Mastercard (Document 10): 9 months for domestic changes; 12 months for cross‑border (foreign‑issued) changes, measured from the final decision.
- Westpac (Document 14): minimum 6 months, effective date no earlier than 1 March 2027, aligned to a first‑of‑month start, and avoiding a Dec–Feb deployment.

Applying both to each cap:

| Cap | Mastercard minimum | Westpac first‑of‑month constraint | Earliest start that satisfies both | |-----|-------------------|-----------------------------------|-----------------------------------| | 0.50 % (domestic) | 9 months → 13 Apr 2027 | First month on/after that, and ≥1 Mar 2027 | 1 May 2027 | | 1.50 % (foreign) | 12 months → 13 Jul 2027 | First month on/after that, and ≥1 Mar 2027 | 1 Aug 2027 |

Day counts from 13 July 2026:

- To 1 May 2027 = 292 days
- To 1 Aug 2027 = 384 days

So, in order (lowest then highest):

292 days later (1 May 2027) and 384 days later (1 August 2027).

- Lowest cap (0.50 % – domestic in‑person commercial credit): 274 days later (13 April 2027)

How this is derived

- Domestic in‑person: 0.50 % (lowest)
- Foreign‑issued online: 1.50 % (highest)

1. Submissions deadline / hypothetical final‑decision date (Document 1):

- 13 July 2026 (12 pm Monday).

1. Mastercard’s minimum lead‑time requests (Document 10, Section “Implementation Timing” and Q9 response):

- Domestic rate changes: minimum 9 months from final announcement → 13 April 2027.
- Cross‑border (foreign‑issued) changes: minimum 12 months from final announcement → 13 July 2027.

1. Westpac’s timing requests (Document 14, Section 3.21):

- Minimum six‑month implementation period, with changes taking effect no earlier than 1 March 2027.

1. Satisfying both issuers for each cap (the later of the two conditions applies):

- Domestic (0.50 %): Mastercard’s 9‑month requirement (13 Apr 2027) is later than Westpac’s 1 Mar 2027 / 6‑month window → 13 April 2027 = 274 days after 13 Jul 2026.
- Foreign‑issued (1.50 %): Mastercard’s 12‑month requirement (13 Jul 2027) dominates Westpac’s request → 13 July 2027 = 365 days after 13 Jul 2026.

So, in order (lowest cap first, highest cap second): 274 days, then 365 days.

Full answer
-----------

Calculating Earliest Start Dates for the Lowest and Highest Proposed Caps

Identifying the Key Inputs

Submission deadline (hypothetical final decision date): 20 July 2026 — the date on which the majority of submissions (ASB, Retail NZ, Revolut, TSB, Restaurant Association) are dated.

Proposed caps (from Restaurant Association submission, Document 15): | Cap | Rate | Type | |---|---|---| | Domestic in-person | 0.50% (lowest) | Domestic | | Foreign-issued in-person | 0.70% | Cross-border | | Domestic online | 0.90% | Domestic | | Foreign-issued online | 1.50% (highest) | Cross-border |

Mastercard's minimum lead times (Document 9): "A minimum of nine months is required for domestic rate changes and twelve months for cross-border changes, from the date of final announcement."

Westpac's timing requests (Document 13): Minimum six months; effective 1 March 2027 at earliest; avoid December–February deployment; first-of-month effective date.

Calculating Start Dates

Lowest cap — 0.50% (domestic in-person)

- Mastercard: 20 July 2026 + 9 months = 20 April 2027
- Westpac first-of-month rule → next 1st = 1 May 2027 (not in Dec–Feb ✓; after 1 March 2027 ✓)
- 285 days from 20 July 2026

Highest cap — 1.50% (foreign-issued online)

- Mastercard: 20 July 2026 + 12 months = 20 July 2027
- Westpac first-of-month rule → next 1st = 1 August 2027 (not in Dec–Feb ✓; after 1 March 2027 ✓)
- 377 days from 20 July 2026

Answer

The lowest cap (0.50%) could first start 285 days later (1 May 2027), and the highest cap (1.50%) could first start 377 days later (1 August 2027).

Full answer
-----------

Finding the Answer

Step 1: Final Decision Date

The Commission's submission deadline was 12pm Monday, 13 July 2026 (Document 1, para X17). The question states the final decision comes on this date.

Step 2: The Lowest and Highest Proposed Caps

From the Commission's Table X1 / Table 3.1 (Document 1):

| Cap | Rate | |-----|------| | Lowest — Domestic commercial credit, in-person | 0.50% | | Highest — Foreign-issued commercial credit, online | 1.50% |

Step 3: Mastercard's Minimum Lead Times (Document 10)

Full answer

Answer: 231 days

Working through the constraints

Final decision date: The submission deadline is 12pm Monday 13 July 2026 (Document 1, para X17). So the hypothetical final decision date is 13 July 2026.

Proposed caps (lowest → highest):

- Lowest: 0.50% (domestic in-person commercial credit)
- Highest: 1.50% (foreign-issued online commercial credit)

Both are implemented together through the same Amendment Pricing Standard (Document 1, para X15).

Westpac's timing requests (Document 13, paras 3.21–3.23)

1. Minimum six-month implementation period from the final decision
2. Changes taking effect from 1 March 2027 at the earliest
3. First-of-month effective date

→ The binding date is 1 March 2027 (satisfies all three: >6 months from 13 July 2026, on/after 1 March 2027, and the 1st of the month).

Mastercard's minimum lead times

ASB's experience (Document 4) shows schemes provided ~3 months from rate confirmation to effective date, with rates confirmed ~2 months after the Commission decision (≈5 months total). This is well within Westpac's 1 March 2027 date, so both constraints are satisfied.

Days calculation: 13 July 2026 → 1 March 2027

| Period | Days | |---|---| | 14–31 July 2026 | 18 | | August | 31 | | September | 30 | | October | 31 | | November | 30 | | December | 31 | | January 2027 | 31 | | February 2027 | 28 | | 1 March 2027 | 1 | | Total | 231 |

Result

Both the lowest cap (0.50%) and the highest cap (1.50%) could first start 231 days after the final decision — on 1 March 2027.

Calculating Earliest Start Dates for the Lowest and Highest Proposed Caps

Identifying Key Facts

Submission deadline / hypothetical decision date: Based on the submission dates in the documents, the latest submission is Westpac's on 21 July 2026 — I take this as the deadline for submissions.

Proposed caps (per the Restaurant Association's summary of the Draft Decision): | Cap | Type | |-----|------| | 0.50% | Domestic, in-person (lowest) | | 0.70% | Foreign-issued, in-person | | 0.90% | Domestic, online | | 1.50% | Foreign-issued, online (highest) |

Mastercard's minimum lead times (Doc 9): "A minimum of nine months is required for domestic rate changes and twelve months for cross-border changes, from the date of final announcement."

Westpac's timing requests (Doc 13):

- Minimum 6-month implementation; changes effective from 1 March 2027 at earliest
- Changes should align with a first-of-month effective date

Applying the Constraints

Lowest cap — 0.50% (domestic in-person)

| Constraint | Earliest date | |---|---| | Mastercard (9 months) | 21 April 2027 | | Westpac (≥ 6 months) | 21 January 2027 | | Westpac (not before 1 Mar 2027) | 1 March 2027 | | Westpac (first of month) | must be 1st of month |

Binding constraint: 21 April 2027 → rounded to 1 May 2027

Days from 21 July 2026 → 1 May 2027 = 284 days

Highest cap — 1.50% (foreign-issued online)

| Constraint | Earliest date | |---|---| | Mastercard (12 months, cross-border) | 21 July 2027 | | Westpac (≥ 6 months) | 21 January 2027 | | Westpac (not before 1 Mar 2027) | 1 March 2027 | | Westpac (first of month) | must be 1st of month |

Binding constraint: 21 July 2027 → rounded to 1 August 2027

Days from 21 July 2026 → 1 August 2027 = 376 days

Answer

The lowest cap (0.50%, domestic in-person) could first start 284 days later (1 May 2027), and the highest cap (1.50%, foreign-issued online) could first start 376 days later (1 August 2027).

Full answer

Calculating the Earliest Start Dates for the Proposed Caps

Key Inputs

Final decision date: 13 July 2026 (the submission deadline: "Please submit your views by 12pm on Monday 13 July 2026", Doc 1, ¶1.21)

Proposed caps (lowest → highest): | Cap | Rate | |---|---| | Lowest: Domestic in-person | 0.50% | | Highest: Foreign-issued online | 1.50% |

Timing Constraints

Mastercard's minimum lead times: The Commission's proposed implementation period is 2–3 months from the final decision (Doc 1, ¶3.80), reflecting the minimum time schemes need. ASB notes the schemes gave acquirers 3 months after bulletin for the July 2025 changes (Doc 4). Either way, well under Westpac's constraint.

Westpac's timing requests (Doc 13, ¶¶3.21–3.23):

1. Minimum 6-month implementation period → earliest 13 January 2027
2. 1 March 2027 at the earliest
3. Avoid December–February implementation
4. First-of-month effective date

Binding Constraint

The most restrictive of these is Westpac's 1 March 2027 (a first-of-month date, outside Dec–Feb, and over 7 months after the decision).

Day Count: 13 July 2026 → 1 March 2027

| Period | Days | |---|---| | 13–31 July 2026 | 18 | | August 2026 | 31 | | September 2026 | 30 | | October 2026 | 31 | | November 2026 | 30 | | December 2026 | 31 | | January 2027 | 31 | | February 2027 | 28 | | 1 March 2027 | 1 | | Total | 231 |

Answer

Both caps are implemented together under the single Amendment Pricing Standard, so the lowest cap (0.50%) and the highest cap (1.50%) would first start on the same date: 231 days after the final decision (1 March 2027), in that order.

3.23 Additionally, any interchange changes should align with a first-of-month effective date. Merchant service fees are generally billed on a monthly cycle, and mid-month changes would create additional complexity where transactions within the same billing period are subject to different pricing. Aligning implementation to the start of a month would support clearer billing, simpler reconciliation and a more effective customer transition."

Westpac wants a minimum six-month implementation period, with changes taking effect from March 1, 2027 at the earliest, and they want it to align with a first-of-month effective date.

Reasoning
---------

To answer this question, I need to find information about:
1. The Commerce Commission's deadline for submissions
2. ASB's requested implementation timeline
3. Westpac's requested implementation timeline
4. Calculate how many days after the submission deadline the caps could first start while satisfying both ASB's and Westpac's timing requests

Let me search through the documents for this information.

First, the submission deadline:
From Document 1 (the main draft decision paper), I can see in section 1.21:
"We are seeking feedback on our draft decision and draft Amendment Pricing Standard by 12pm on Monday 13 July 2026 and will allow two weeks after publication of submissions for cross-submissions."

So the submission deadline was 12pm on Monday, July 13, 2026.

Now, let's find ASB's requested implementation timeline.

From Document 4 (ASB Bank Limited submission):
"ASB Recommendation 3: Set an implementation date of no earlier than 8 months from the announcement date and Commission to direct the schemes to confirm applicable rates and criteria no later than 2 months from announcement date to allow acquirers 6 months for implementation."

Also: "Our preference would be for the Commission to set an implementation date of no earlier than 8 months from the announcement date and for the Commission to direct the schemes to confirm applicable rates and criteria no later than 2 months from announcement date to allow acquirers 6 months for implementation."

So ASB wants implementation date no earlier than 8 months from the announcement date (which would be the final decision date).

Now for Westpac's requested implementation timeline.

From Document 14 (Westpac submission):
"3.21 December implementation carries operational risks that may negatively affect customers. Interchange changes require pricing, billing, systems, scheme and customer communication changes, all of which need adequate time for implementation, testing and transition. Westpac considers that a minimum six-month implementation period, with changes taking effect from 1 March 2027 at the earliest, would better support orderly delivery, reduce customer disruption and maintain the stability of critical payments infrastructure."

"3.22 Implementation over the December to February period would introduce additional operational complexity. This period typically coincides with heightened transaction volumes, including holiday spending and tourism activity, as well as industry technology change freezes and reduced resource availability. As a result, the capacity to safely implement, monitor and remediate material pricing, billing and systems changes is more constrained than at other times of the year."

"3.23 Additionally, any interchange changes should align with a first-of-month effective date. Merchant service fees are generally billed on a monthly cycle, and mid-month changes would create additional complexity where transactions within the same billing period are subject to different pricing. Aligning implementation to the start of a month would support clearer billing, simpler reconciliation and a more effective customer transition."

So Westpac wants:
- Minimum six-month implementation period
- Changes taking effect from March 1, 2027 at the earliest
- Implementation should align with first-of-month effective date
- Avoid December-February period

"3.21 December implementation carries operational risks that may negatively affect customers. Interchange changes require pricing, billing, systems, scheme and customer communication changes, all of which need adequate time for implementation, testing and transition. Westpac considers that a minimum six-month implementation period, with changes taking effect from 1 March 2027 at the earliest, would better support orderly delivery, reduce customer disruption and maintain the stability of critical payments infrastructure."

"3.22 Implementation over the December to February period would introduce additional operational complexity. This period typically coincides with heightened transaction volumes, including holiday spending and tourism activity, as well as industry technology change freezes and reduced resource availability. As a result, the capacity to safely implement, monitor and remediate material pricing, billing and systems changes is more constrained than at other times of the year."

"3.23 Additionally, any interchange changes should align with a first-of-month effective date. Merchant service fees are generally billed on a monthly cycle, and mid-month changes would create additional complexity where transactions within the same billing period are subject to different pricing. Aligning implementation to the start of a month would support clearer billing, simpler reconciliation and a more effective customer transition."

So Westpac wants:
- Minimum 6-month implementation period
- Changes taking effect from March 1, 2027 at the earliest
- Implementation should align with first-of-month effective date
- Avoid December-February period

Until these details have been received, which is generally through a scheme bulletin, our ability to understand the extent of the changes or the implementation effort and timeframes is limited. For the domestic rate changes announced in July 2025, the details of the changes being made by the schemes was allowing us 3 months to complete implementation work. As discussed with the Commission at that time, this made implementation challenging and very inefficient, as due to the timing of scheme decisions.

Two to three months is therefore insufficient to make the required system changes, and a compressed timeframe increases the risk of incorrect merchant billing, which could result in merchants being overcharged requiring subsequent remediation."

And: "Any interchange rate changes would require ASB to:
(a) make material changes to the third-party merchant system, including and implementation of the new rates;
(b) update pricing models, financial monitoring, reporting and controls, with some activities dependent on;
(c) redesign controls to monitor compliance with the Retail Payments System Act; and
(d) communicate the changes to affected customers."

- Then 6 months for implementation

Announcement date = final decision date = July 13, 2026 (hypothetical).

July 13 + 8 months = March 13, 2027.

Westpac: "Westpac considers that a minimum six-month implementation period, with changes taking effect from 1 March 2027 at the earliest, would better support orderly delivery... Additionally, any interchange changes should align with a first-of-month effective date."

So Westpac requires:
- Implementation date >= March 1, 2027
- Implementation date is first of month
- (Implicitly >= 6 months from decision, but March 1 is > 6 months)

Since March 1, 2027 < March 13, 2027, the binding constraint for the date is ASB's March 13, but with Westpac's first-of-month requirement, so April 1, 2027.

Days from July 13, 2026 to April 1, 2027 = 262 days.
